Like… I still like the same stuff, and if I actually get myself to do it, it’s usually fine. I haven’t suddenly stopped caring about my friends or school activities or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more that sometimes everything feels like too much to organize, so I’ll put things off or not feel like making the effort in the moment. But the interest is still there, I think. I’m not sitting there thinking, “I don’t enjoy this anymore.” It’s more like I’m already tired or distracted before I even start.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not hopeless exactly, or at least I don’t think that’s the word I’d use. I’m still getting up and going to class and work at the shop and doing the things I’m supposed to do, mostly. But I have felt more down than normal, definitely more worn out and kind of discouraged.\n\nIt’s tied up with everything at home, I think. Since my sister and her baby moved back in, the house just feels crowded and tense all the time, and I feel like I’m already on edge before anything even happens. Then I’ll snap over some small thing, like noise or someone being in the way, and afterward I feel bad about it. So it’s not like I’m depressed every single minute, but over the last couple weeks I’ve been carrying this drained, low feeling around a lot more than usual.\n\nAnd it’s showing up in other places too. I’ll get to class or to work already feeling like I don’t have much patience left, and I’ve missed a couple deadlines, which makes me feel worse because I usually try to stay on top of that stuff. I also backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep everything straight in my head. So… yeah, I’ve been down, but more in that stressed and worn-down way than feeling completely hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant loss of energy or feeling more fatigued than usual, even when you haven’t done much physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the bigger things I’ve noticed. It’s not like I’m doing some huge physical job all day and then feeling tired in a way that makes sense — although the shop can be tiring — it’s more like I’m already drained before I even get there.\n\nSince my sister and her baby moved back home, the house just feels crowded all the time, and I feel like I’m on alert even when nothing is really happening. So I’ll wake up or get ready for class already kind of worn down, like I’ve used up energy just being at home. Then by the time I’m at class or at the shop, I can still function, but it feels like everything takes more effort than it should.\n\nI’m still getting by, mostly, but it’s been enough that I’ve missed a couple deadlines, which isn’t normal for me. And I backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep everything organized in my head. So yeah… I wouldn’t say I’m completely exhausted all the time, but I’m definitely more tired and run down than usual.\n\nDoctor: Has this been affecting your ability to function in your usual social, school, or work activities in a noticeable way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it has, though I’m still technically doing the main stuff. I’m still going to class, and I’m still showing up for work, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped functioning or anything. But it’s noticeable to me because I’m usually pretty good about keeping track of what needs to get done.\n\nI’ve turned in a couple assignments late, which doesn’t sound huge maybe, but for me it’s not normal. It’s like I know the deadline is there, and then I keep thinking I’ll get to it, and suddenly I’m behind. And I had to drop helping with the community theater set project, which I didn’t really want to do, because I just couldn’t keep up with everything at once. I was worried I’d end up letting people down more if I stayed on and couldn’t follow through.\n\nSo… yeah, I’m still managing, but not as well as I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worse about yourself lately, like you're not good enough or that you're failing at things you should be able to handle?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I feel like I’m not good enough exactly. Maybe sometimes I get frustrated with myself, like, “Why can’t I just handle this better?” especially when I don’t keep up with something I normally would. But it’s not this constant thing where I’m sitting around thinking I’m a failure.\n\nIt’s more like I feel disappointed in myself in the moment, and then I sort of replay it too much. Like if I mess something up or don’t handle things well, I’ll think about it longer than I probably need to. But I still know I’m trying, if that makes sense. I don’t feel worthless or anything like that. Just… not as on top of things as I usually expect myself to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that activities you usually enjoy feel less rewarding or that you get less pleasure out of them than before?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, exactly. Like… I still enjoy things when I actually get myself to do them. It’s not like everything feels empty or pointless or like I can’t feel good about anything.\n\nI guess sometimes it takes me longer to settle into enjoying something, because I’ll be distracted or kind of thinking about other stuff in the background. But if I’m with people I like, or doing something I normally like, I can still have a decent time. It’s more that I’m not always in the right headspace at first, not that the activity itself has stopped being enjoyable.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve really noticed a big loss of pleasure. Not in a major way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re less motivated to do things you normally have to get done, like schoolwork, chores, or even just getting out of bed in the morning?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, at least with stuff I have to get done. I’m still getting up and going to class and work, so it’s not like I’m staying in bed all day or just not showing up. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is. But the motivation feels lower, or maybe it’s more like it takes way more effort to start things.\n\nWith schoolwork especially, I’ll know I need to do an assignment, and instead of just sitting down and doing it like I normally would, I kind of keep putting it off and telling myself I’ll do it later. Then later turns into too late, and I’ve turned in a couple assignments late because of that. That’s not really like me, so it bothers me.\n\nAnd outside of school, I had to drop out of helping with the community theater set project. I actually wanted to keep doing it, but I just couldn’t keep up with everything, and I felt like if I stayed involved I’d end up not pulling my weight. So, yeah, I’m still functioning, but I’m definitely not keeping up the way I usually do.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, and was there a specific time or event when you noticed these feelings really starting to affect you?\n\nPatient: It’s been about a month, I think. Not like I woke up one day and everything was suddenly different, but if I’m trying to pin it down, it really started after my sister and her baby moved back into the house.\n\nAt first I thought it was just an adjustment thing, like the house being busier and everything feeling kind of shifted around. But after a week or two I started noticing it was actually affecting me more than I expected. So… yeah, about a month now, since they moved back in.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you experienced five or more of the following nearly every day: depressed mood, loss of interest or pleasure, significant weight loss or gain, insomnia or hypersomnia, psychomotor agitation or retardation, fatigue, feelings of worthlessness, difficulty concentrating, or recurrent thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. I mean, when you list it all like that, it’s kind of hard for me to mentally check each thing off neatly, but I wouldn’t say it’s been five or more of those things nearly every day.\n\nI’ve had some rough days, definitely, and I’ve felt more off than usual, but not in that really constant, across-the-board way you’re describing. Like I said, I still can enjoy things once I’m actually doing them, so I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest or pleasure completely. And I don’t think there’s been some major change like… I don’t know, a huge weight change or anything like that.\n\nMostly it’s just that things have felt harder to keep up with, and I’ve been more distracted and slower to get going. But five or more of those symptoms every day for two weeks? No, I don’t think that fits.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your reactions to your sister and her baby moving back home have been more intense or longer-lasting than you would expect, or than most people would typically experience in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s part of why I came in, honestly. Like, I know anyone would be stressed if the house suddenly changed and there was a baby there and more noise and less space and everything kind of revolving around that. So I don’t want to act like my reaction is totally shocking or like nobody else would feel it.\n\nBut for me it feels like it’s gone past just being annoyed or needing a few days to adjust. Ever since my sister and her baby moved back home, I’ve been more on edge than I expected, and more down than I really want to admit. I’ll snap over little stuff at the house, like things being in the way or noise when I’m trying to focus, and then afterward I feel bad because it’s not like they’re doing anything wrong exactly. It’s there, yeah, but it’s kind of mixed in with being on edge and overwhelmed.\n\nLike, the reason I came in is because ever since my sister and her baby moved back home, I haven’t felt like myself. I’m more tense at home, and I snap over little stuff—noise, things being in the way, just normal house stuff that probably shouldn’t bother me that much. Most of what I notice is just the general tension in the house—more noise, less privacy, everyone trying to work around each other. I still get hungry, and I’m still eating. I wouldn’t say food has become a major issue or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your ability to handle school, work, or social responsibilities has dropped below what you’d normally expect of yourself, even if you’re still managing to do most of what you need to do?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that’s probably fair to say. I’m still doing the main things, like I’m still going to class and I’m still showing up for work, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped functioning or anything. Yeah, I do. I mean, it starts at home, like that’s definitely where it’s coming from, but once I’m away from the house it doesn’t just shut off.

So… yeah, I’ve been down, but more in that stressed and worn-down way than feeling completely hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant loss of energy or feeling more fatigued than usual, even when you haven’t done much physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s actually been one of the bigger things I’ve noticed. It’s not like I’m doing some huge physical job all day and then feeling tired in a way that makes sense — although the shop can be tiring — it’s more like I’m already drained before I even get there.\n\nSince my sister and her baby moved back home, the house just feels crowded all the time, and I feel like I’m on alert even when nothing is really happening. So I’ll wake up or get ready for class already kind of worn down, like I’ve used up energy just being at home. Then by the time I’m at class or at the shop, I can still function, but it feels like everything takes more effort than it should.\n\nI’m still getting by, mostly, but it’s been enough that I’ve missed a couple deadlines, which isn’t normal for me. And I backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep everything organized in my head. So yeah… I wouldn’t say I’m completely exhausted all the time, but I’m definitely more tired and run down than usual.\n\nDoctor: Has this been affecting your ability to function in your usual social, school, or work activities in a noticeable way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it has, though I’m still technically doing the main stuff. I’m still going to class, and I’m still showing up for work, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped functioning or anything. But it’s noticeable to me because I’m usually pretty good about keeping track of what needs to get done.\n\nI’ve turned in a couple assignments late, which doesn’t sound huge maybe, but for me it’s not normal. It’s like I know the deadline is there, and then I keep thinking I’ll get to it, and suddenly I’m behind. And I had to drop helping with the community theater set project, which I didn’t really want to do, because I just couldn’t keep up with everything at once. I was worried I’d end up letting people down more if I stayed on and couldn’t follow through.\n\nSo… yeah, I’m still managing, but not as well as I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worse about yourself lately, like you're not good enough or that you're failing at things you should be able to handle?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I feel like I’m not good enough exactly. Maybe sometimes I get frustrated with myself, like, “Why can’t I just handle this better?” especially when I don’t keep up with something I normally would. But it’s not this constant thing where I’m sitting around thinking I’m a failure.\n\nIt’s more like I feel disappointed in myself in the moment, and then I sort of replay it too much. Like if I mess something up or don’t handle things well, I’ll think about it longer than I probably need to. But I still know I’m trying, if that makes sense. I don’t feel worthless or anything like that. Just… not as on top of things as I usually expect myself to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that activities you usually enjoy feel less rewarding or that you get less pleasure out of them than before?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, exactly. Like… I still enjoy things when I actually get myself to do them. It’s not like everything feels empty or pointless or like I can’t feel good about anything.\n\nI guess sometimes it takes me longer to settle into enjoying something, because I’ll be distracted or kind of thinking about other stuff in the background. But if I’m with people I like, or doing something I normally like, I can still have a decent time. It’s more that I’m not always in the right headspace at first, not that the activity itself has stopped being enjoyable.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve really noticed a big loss of pleasure. Not in a major way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re less motivated to do things you normally have to get done, like schoolwork, chores, or even just getting out of bed in the morning?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, at least with stuff I have to get done. I’m still getting up and going to class and work, so it’s not like I’m staying in bed all day or just not showing up. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is. But the motivation feels lower, or maybe it’s more like it takes way more effort to start things.\n\nWith schoolwork especially, I’ll know I need to do an assignment, and instead of just sitting down and doing it like I normally would, I kind of keep putting it off and telling myself I’ll do it later. Then later turns into too late, and I’ve turned in a couple assignments late because of that. That’s not really like me, so it bothers me.\n\nAnd outside of school, I had to drop out of helping with the community theater set project. I actually wanted to keep doing it, but I just couldn’t keep up with everything, and I felt like if I stayed involved I’d end up not pulling my weight. So, yeah, I’m still functioning, but I’m definitely not keeping up the way I usually do.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, and was there a specific time or event when you noticed these feelings really starting to affect you?\n\nPatient: It’s been about a month, I think. Not like I woke up one day and everything was suddenly different, but if I’m trying to pin it down, it really started after my sister and her baby moved back into the house.\n\nAt first I thought it was just an adjustment thing, like the house being busier and everything feeling kind of shifted around. But after a week or two I started noticing it was actually affecting me more than I expected. So… yeah, about a month now, since they moved back in.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you experienced five or more of the following nearly every day: depressed mood, loss of interest or pleasure, significant weight loss or gain, insomnia or hypersomnia, psychomotor agitation or retardation, fatigue, feelings of worthlessness, difficulty concentrating, or recurrent thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. I mean, when you list it all like that, it’s kind of hard for me to mentally check each thing off neatly, but I wouldn’t say it’s been five or more of those things nearly every day.\n\nI’ve had some rough days, definitely, and I’ve felt more off than usual, but not in that really constant, across-the-board way you’re describing. Like I said, I still can enjoy things once I’m actually doing them, so I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest or pleasure completely. And I don’t think there’s been some major change like… I don’t know, a huge weight change or anything like that.\n\nMostly it’s just that things have felt harder to keep up with, and I’ve been more distracted and slower to get going. But five or more of those symptoms every day for two weeks? No, I don’t think that fits.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your reactions to your sister and her baby moving back home have been more intense or longer-lasting than you would expect, or than most people would typically experience in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s part of why I came in, honestly. Like, I know anyone would be stressed if the house suddenly changed and there was a baby there and more noise and less space and everything kind of revolving around that. So I don’t want to act like my reaction is totally shocking or like nobody else would feel it.\n\nBut for me it feels like it’s gone past just being annoyed or needing a few days to adjust. Ever since my sister and her baby moved back home, I’ve been more on edge than I expected, and more down than I really want to admit. I’ll snap over little stuff at the house, like things being in the way or noise when I’m trying to focus, and then afterward I feel bad because it’s not like they’re doing anything wrong exactly. It’s just… I’m already tense, so small things hit me harder.\n\nAnd then I’m showing up to class or to the shop already drained, like I used up all my patience before the day even started. I’m still getting by, I’m not totally falling apart, but I’m not handling things the way I normally would. I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I couldn’t keep everything straight. So yeah, I guess it does feel more intense or longer-lasting than I would’ve expected for just “family moved back in.”\n\nDoctor: Would you say that feeling down or low has been the main way this has affected you, more than things like anxiety, irritability, or just feeling overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say feeling down is the main thing, exactly. It’s there, yeah, but it’s kind of mixed in with being on edge and overwhelmed.\n\nLike, the reason I came in is because ever since my sister and her baby moved back home, I haven’t felt like myself. I’m more tense at home, and I snap over little stuff—noise, things being in the way, just normal house stuff that probably shouldn’t bother me that much. Then afterward I feel bad about it, because I know they’re not doing anything wrong on purpose.\n\nAnd then I’ll go to class or the shop already feeling drained, like I’ve spent all my energy just getting through the morning at home. I’m still functioning, I guess—I’m getting by—but not as well as usual. I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep everything straight.\n\nSo… it’s not only feeling low. It’s more like being stressed and on edge has been wearing me down, and then that turns into feeling down too.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sister’s mood or behavior since the baby was born that have added to the tension at home?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I mean, I can tell she’s tired, because… obviously, there’s a baby, and the whole house kind of runs around the baby’s schedule now. But I don’t know if I’ve noticed a specific change in her mood or behavior beyond what I’d expect from that.\n\nLike, I don’t want to sit here and make it sound like I’ve been carefully observing her or that I know what’s going on with her internally. Most of what I notice is just the general tension in the house—more noise, less privacy, everyone trying to work around each other. She might be stressed too, but I couldn’t really say, “Oh, she’s been acting this certain way since the baby was born.” I don’t know. I’ve mostly been focused on how I’m reacting to everything, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or eating habits lately, like eating a lot more or a lot less than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any big obvious way. Like, I haven’t been tracking it or anything, so I’m trying to think if there’s been some pattern I’m missing, but… no, I don’t think I’m eating a lot more or a lot less than usual.\n\nSome days are a little weird just because the house is hectic, so I might eat later than I meant to or just grab whatever’s easy, but that feels more like the schedule being off than my appetite changing. I still get hungry, and I’m still eating. I wouldn’t say food has become a major issue or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your ability to handle school, work, or social responsibilities has dropped below what you’d normally expect of yourself, even if you’re still managing to do most of what you need to do?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that’s probably fair to say. I’m still doing the main things, like I’m still going to class and I’m still showing up for work, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped functioning or anything. But compared to how I usually handle stuff, it’s definitely dropped off.\n\nI’ve turned in a couple assignments late, which I really don’t like. That’s not usually how I am—I mean, I’m not perfect, but I can normally keep track of deadlines better than that. Lately it’s like I’ll know something is due, and then everything at home and work and school piles together, and by the time I sit down to actually do it, I’m already behind.\n\nAnd I had to back out of the community theater set project, too. I’d agreed to help with building the set, and normally I’d be able to manage something like that, especially because it’s the kind of hands-on thing I actually like doing. But I just couldn’t keep up with everything at once, and I didn’t want to be the person who kept saying I’d show up and then not be reliable. So I dropped it, which felt pretty bad.\n\nSo, yeah—I’m getting through the basics, but it’s below what I’d normally expect from myself.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your ability to cope with daily responsibilities and stressors has been noticeably worse than it was before your sister and her baby moved back home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I would say it’s been noticeably worse. Not like I’m completely falling apart or anything, but compared to before my sister and the baby moved back in, I feel like I don’t have as much room in my head to deal with normal stuff.\n\nI came in because since all that changed at home, I’ve been more on edge and kind of down more than usual. Little things at the house get to me faster than they used to, and I’ll snap over stuff that probably isn’t that big of a deal. Then I feel bad about it, but I’m already irritated or worn out, and it just keeps carrying over.\n\nAnd it’s not just at home. I’ll show up to class or to the shop already drained, like I haven’t even started the day and I’m already behind somehow. I’m still doing the main things, so I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but I’m not handling everything the way I normally would. I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep everything straight anymore. That’s the kind of thing I usually would’ve been able to manage, so yeah… it feels worse than before.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these symptoms only in relation to the stress at home, or do they also happen in other parts of your life, like at school or work, even when things are going normally there?\n\nPatient: It started because of the stuff at home, definitely. Like, before my sister and the baby moved back in, I wasn’t feeling this on edge all the time, at least not in the same way. So I do think it’s tied to that change more than anything.\n\nBut it doesn’t stay only inside the house, if that makes sense. I’ll have a rough morning or just be around all the noise and stress at home, and then I’ll show up to class or the shop already drained, like I’m carrying it with me. So even if school or work is mostly normal that day, I’m not really showing up the same way I usually would.\n\nThat’s where it’s been causing problems, I guess. I’m still getting by, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep everything straight. It’s not like school or work suddenly became terrible on their own—it’s more like the stress at home has been spilling over into everything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice these feelings of being worn down and on edge only when you're dealing with the situation at home, or do they also come up in other parts of your life even when things are going smoothly?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly connected to what’s going on at home, I think. Like, that’s where it starts. Ever since my sister and her baby moved back in, I’ve just been more on edge and kind of down than I’m used to, and I notice myself snapping over little things around the house—stuff that maybe wouldn’t have bothered me as much before.\n\nBut it doesn’t totally stay there. I’ll leave the house and go to class or to the shop already feeling worn out, like I used up all my patience before the day even really started. So even if school or work is going normally, I’m still carrying that stress with me, and it makes it harder to keep up.\n\nI’m still getting by, like I’m not just skipping everything or giving up, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I couldn’t keep everything straight. That’s the part that makes me feel like it’s spilling over, not just something that happens when I’m physically at home.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice these symptoms even when you're away from home, such as at school or work, even when things there are going smoothly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I mean, it starts at home, like that’s definitely where it’s coming from, but once I’m away from the house it doesn’t just shut off.\n\nI’ll go to class or to the shop already feeling drained, like I’m still carrying all the noise and tension from home with me. Even if nothing bad is happening at school or work, I’m still more on edge than I used to be, and it’s harder to focus or keep things straight. I’m still getting by, so it’s not like I’m completely falling apart, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I ended up backing out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t manage everything in my head.\n\nSo yeah, even when things are technically going smoothly outside the house, I’m not really feeling normal there either."
